# Predestination in the Parables of Jesus

**[00:00:00]** Hey everybody, welcome to the show.

**[00:00:23]** My name is Tudor Alexander and this is the Dance of Life podcast.

**[00:00:26]** This is part eight of the Once Saved, Always Saved series.

**[00:00:31]** We're talking about predestination in the parables of Jesus.

**[00:00:34]** So if you haven't tuned in in the last episodes, we talked about things like total depravity,

**[00:00:41]** God doing the work through irresistible grace, and basically throughout history,

**[00:00:47]** coming to people that he's chosen to save, the elect, and working in their lives despite their doubt,

**[00:00:54]** despite their fear.

**[00:00:55]** Despite their failures throughout many cases throughout the Bible.

**[00:01:00]** We talked about predestination of both good and evil.

**[00:01:03]** We talked about the elect, how that's a teaching throughout the Bible,

**[00:01:07]** both the Old Testament and obviously revealed and fulfilled in the New Testament.

**[00:01:11]** We talked about the reprobate, which are people who God chose to pass over,

**[00:01:15]** who he did not save.

**[00:01:17]** The wicked, right, that are created for the day of destruction and they have their own purpose.

**[00:01:22]** Last episode, we talked about the incarnation.

**[00:01:25]** And how every aspect of Christ's life on earth was predestined.

**[00:01:30]** You know, we went through multiple examples in scripture.

**[00:01:33]** We also talked about how we're being conformed to the image of Christ,

**[00:01:36]** how we were made in the image of God.

**[00:01:39]** And obviously Adam was a type of the one to come.

**[00:01:43]** So we were this whole predestination of God being in us,

**[00:01:46]** being conformed to the image of Christ with the spirit in us and having our own bodies,

**[00:01:51]** but at the same time being vessels that God is acting through.

**[00:01:54]** Right?

**[00:01:55]** That whole reality was predestined.

**[00:01:57]** And that's very visible by studying the incarnation of Jesus.

**[00:02:00]** So we looked at that in the last episode.

**[00:02:03]** In the one before that, we talked about the predestination of evil,

**[00:02:07]** which was a very long episode.

**[00:02:08]** So I encourage you to listen to that one in chunks, but there's so much information.

**[00:02:14]** We looked at various books of the Bible, like the book of Job, book of Ezekiel,

**[00:02:18]** you know, countless scriptures in how God takes ownership of everything that happens,

**[00:02:24]** both the good and the bad.

**[00:02:25]** And, and sorry, there's a motorcycle just running around in my neighborhood all the time.

**[00:02:32]** He takes credit for the good and the bad that happens in history.

**[00:02:36]** And every time he does so, he does so with a purpose.

**[00:02:40]** There's a purpose behind everything that happens.

**[00:02:43]** And so all this stuff that we've been talking about,

**[00:02:47]** we have a couple more episodes that I have planned in this series.

**[00:02:50]** This episode is talking about the parables of Jesus.

**[00:02:53]** And then the next episode,

**[00:02:55]** I'm going to talk about various questions that come up for people that are struggling

**[00:03:00]** with this idea of predestination and election.

**[00:03:02]** So I'm just going to go through a bunch of frequently asked questions.

**[00:03:05]** And after that, the final episode, I don't know how long that one's going to be.

**[00:03:08]** Maybe it's going to be another really long episode, but either way,

**[00:03:10]** that one I've devoted to strictly objections.

**[00:03:14]** So challenge verses that seem to question eternal security,

**[00:03:18]** or it seemed to question that you can lose your salvation, you know,

**[00:03:22]** or seem to teach that, you know, it's up to us in some way to, you know,

**[00:03:24]** to choose to have faith and then we're saved, that kind of thing.

**[00:03:29]** So all those are coming up, but this is pretty much the last, you know,

**[00:03:34]** we kind of put all these together through the very words of Jesus himself,

**[00:03:36]** through his parables.

**[00:03:37]** And I think parables are fascinating.

**[00:03:40]** You know, they're so interesting because they give us a glimpse into the mind of God.

**[00:03:44]** You know, everything that Jesus talked, he talked in parables, right?

**[00:03:48]** He was teaching in parables.

**[00:03:49]** And that was for one reason, because the, the elect would understand,

**[00:03:54]** the people who needed to understand would understand.

**[00:03:56]** And the people who hated him, right?

**[00:03:58]** The Pharisees, the people who were reprobate would just hate him even more.

**[00:04:03]** They wouldn't understand.

**[00:04:04]** He was sort of concealing things in plain sight in a way.

**[00:04:08]** And another thing for us believers today, that's important with parables is

**[00:04:13]** they're proof of everything we've been talking about, right?

**[00:04:16]** So this is not, I hope that if you have tuned in, in the last several episodes,

**[00:04:21]** you see that I've done a, I've done my best.

**[00:04:24]** I've done my best to be as, as thorough as possible with this topic and to provide you

**[00:04:28]** with as much scripture as possible, that these things are not things that are, we're making

**[00:04:32]** up, right?

**[00:04:33]** So to, to have eternal security in your salvation, to believe in irresistible grace, that it

**[00:04:38]** takes an act of God to change the human heart.

**[00:04:40]** And when it, when that happens, you can't undo that, right?

**[00:04:45]** To believe that there is an elect that God has chosen and there's people that he's chosen

**[00:04:49]** not to save.

**[00:04:50]** All these things are very biblically sound and they're very established.

**[00:04:53]** And so.

**[00:04:54]** Parables are just another layer of proof for that as hopefully you'll soon see.

**[00:05:00]** And again, we can see the mind of God because parables are like metaphors.

**[00:05:04]** They're like living metaphors of, of things that happen.

**[00:05:08]** So a metaphor, like when you, when you compare something to something else, right?

**[00:05:11]** It's a static picture.

**[00:05:14]** But the thing that's really interesting about parables is, you know, things there's multiple

**[00:05:19]** things being represented and they're interacting with one another.

**[00:05:23]** So it's sort of like this.

**[00:05:23]** It's this living picture, which is, I think just super fascinating.

**[00:05:27]** So there's so much to talk about parables.

**[00:05:34]** The coming kingdom of heaven, the final judgment.

**[00:05:37]** There's a lot of themes revolving around the kingdom.

**[00:05:45]** kingdom, whether it's the coming kingdom at the final judgment or the, you know, the growth
